## 2026-10-07 12:34 PT / 19:34 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — NEW ANOMALY: STRANDED on-chain job **#1058** (escrow 512e9 locked, provider=null, NOT in AP/store) = **concurrent-create id-misattribution in the 0c5c6bb confirm logic**; NO restart, NO code change (fast path ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE, unreachable)
**Root-cause-first:** unit state + provider/AP journals + direct chain reads + AP/provider stores reviewed BEFORE any action; fault-grep (panic/fatal/deadlock/ERROR/1010/1014) last 15m across gpn-provider/accesspoint/fleet = **0 hits**; only benign idempotent `healed leaked Pending` lines ⇒ **no restart**. Fast-path flags APPROVED-ON, intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` provider.env PID 1321305; `GPN_FAST_AP=1` ~/.gpn/accesspoint.env) — not reverted/re-defaulted. Tree clean.
**Probe (text):** POST /v1/chat/completions :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-**1059**`, coherent 270-char answer, **WALL 9.16s (OVER 5s target — known block-time-bound confirm-wait)**, exactly 1 claim/request (journal `claiming #1059` 12:23:26 → `done`). AP id 1059 == chain id (desync fix holding).
**NEW ANOMALY — stranded #1058 (concrete evidence):** on-chain `ComputeMarketplace::Jobs[1058]` present, `state=Pending`, `JobEscrow[1058]=512000000000` (≈**0.512 GPN LOCKED**), `JobRequestor[1058]=0xa8d73…c05079` (the AP account), `JobProvider[1058]=null`, `JobBilling[1058].actual_tokens=0`. BUT `Jobs[1058]` is **ABSENT from `~/.gpn/assignments.json` (AP store)** and **ABSENT from `~/.gpn/llm_store.json` (provider store)** — i.e. an on-chain job with **no client assignment and no provider processing**. Corroborated by journal: `#1057` claimed **twice** (12:19:37 **and** 12:19:44, two inferences) while `#1058` is never claimed. `NextJobId=1060` (best #84339); AP `/jobs` total 1059 {Completed 803, Cancelled 243, Failed 1, **Pending 12**} = documented baseline {305,306,345,568,656,683,684,685,687,709}+#988 **PLUS new #1058**. Stable >15 min (re-checked t+~10m and t+~16m: still Pending) ⇒ **not self-healing** (provider reconcile can only heal ids it has an off-chain `kind=llm,status=done` entry for — #1058 has none ⇒ never healed).
**WHY (root cause, from 0c5c6bb diff):** the AP confirm loop labels the job `real = after - 1` where `after = chain NextJobId` and `next_before` was read *before* the create. Under TWO concurrent creates (two crons probing ~7s apart), request B reads `next_before=1057`; request A's create lands first → B's first poll sees `after=1058>1057` and **attributes A's advance to itself**, labelling B as **1057** (already A's), `reseed_alloc(1058)`, and B's own on-chain job (**1058**) is left with **no label/assignment** (`assignments[1057]` overwritten). Result = on-chain escrow-locked orphan #1058 + double-processed #1057. That is exactly the observed shape (2 claims of 1057, 0 of 1058, store missing 1058). This is a *sibling-attribution* hole in the new confirm logic (the old code's `id==predicted` guard was removed by 0c5c6bb); it is **not** a lost/phantom create (the create *did* land).
**FIX: none applied this run** — correct fix lives in the APPROVED fast path (`gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_scheduler.rs` confirm block) ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE + rebuild + AP restart; `sessions_list` shows only this cron ⇒ **no MAIN reviewer reachable**. No restart (no hang/deadlock), no state wipe, no workaround.
**PROPOSAL (WHAT/WHY/RISK/ROLLBACK — small + reversible, needs review+build):** WHAT: stop inferring our id as `after-1`; identify OUR created job explicitly — after create, scan `NextJobId` from `next_before..after` for the id whose `JobRequestor==AP` and `workload_hash==ours`, label with that; if none matches within the bound, mark FAILED (do NOT fall back to `predicted`) so we never mislabel a sibling. Serialize creates per-AP (mutex) as belt-and-braces. WHY: closes the sibling-attribution hole; ends escrow-locked orphans + duplicate processing. RISK: low — AP-only, read-only extra confirm; no chain/genesis/wasm change. ROLLBACK: `git revert <commit>`; env flags untouched.
**Escalate:** to JEREMY (approval authority) — this is a **funds-relevant** correctness bug (locked escrow) and also the concrete driver for open item **(8)/q8 pallet `expire_job`** (a permissionless timeout is the only recovery for already-stranded ids like #1058). Root cause is decided; only approval authority is missing.
**Blocked/open (unchanged):** (a) `submit_fast` retry-once/nonce-refresh; (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8); (c) RPC-readiness gate; (e) diffusion silent-wedge instrumentation; (f) item 6 Windows; (g) mainnet parity; (h) create-confirm latency vs <5s target. STATUS: CORE GREEN, DESYNC FIXED, **NEW: stranded #1058 + concurrent-create misattribution / NOT DONE**.

- **WHAT:** (1) In `submit_fast` / `create_job_fast`, after an Ok (pool-accepted) create, **verify inclusion** of the predicted id (`client.get_job_state(predicted)` within a bounded ~1-block window); if absent, **tear down the nonce stream and retry once** with a fresh nonce before declaring success. (2) On any Ok-but-unconfirmed or allocation-mismatch, **re-seed the local allocator from chain** (`NEXT_JOB_ID = chain_next-1` before next alloc) so the AP id can't stay permanently ahead. (3) Do NOT write the assignment / return success until inclusion is confirmed.
- **WHY:** closes the silent-create hole on the Ok branch and stops the persistent off-by-one desync; keeps the fast path (no full-block wait) — confirmation is bounded and only on the create path.
- **RISK:** low — AP-only, read-only confirmation (+1 retry on the rare lost tx); no chain/genesis/wasm/tokenomics change.
- **ROLLBACK:** `git revert <commit>`; env flags untouched.
**Open (unchanged + this now evidence-backed):** (a) fast-AP create/submit_fast **retry-once + nonce-refresh + inclusion check** (THIS run's phantom #1003; the 09:08 #988; #709/#719/#720); (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8) + #711 race; (c) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed; (d) explorer subxt metadata-refresh; (d2) GET /jobs O(n) sequential RPC (~15s); (e) item 6 Windows; (f) mainnet parity. **Root-cause-first:** reviewed provider+AP journal, chain `/jobs`, node block log BEFORE acting; no hang/deadlock/panic in logs → **no restart** (restart would drop the janitor in-memory set → more orphans). (2) POST /v1/chat/completions :8092 (llama3.2:1b, permissionless claim/escrow/settlement prompt) **immediately after** → HTTP200 but body `{"content":"[gpn error] could not create job"}` (`chatcmpl-gpn-988`), **WALL 2.10s**. (3) clean single re-probe → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-989` (model returned a short refusal = model-content behaviour, job completed normally), **WALL 4.29s (<5s MET)**. journalctl fault-grep (panic/fatal/deadlock/ERROR/1010/storm) provider+accesspoint last 20m = **0 hits**.
**EVIDENCE / ROOT CAUSE (concrete):** at the instant AP answered, chain `NextJobId`-derived max job id = **987** and **#988 did not exist**; seconds later #988 **appeared on-chain as `Pending`** (max id 988), and it has **no provider assignment** (provider never claimed it; not in provider journal) and remains **Pending >5 min** (`/job/988` → `state:"Pending", provider:null`) → a **NEW persistent orphan** (not in documented baseline {305,306,345,568,656,683,684,685,687,709}).
Exact mechanism (from `gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_scheduler.rs` fast-AP branch ~L626–665 + `chain/mod.rs submit_fast` L871): `next_before = get_next_job_id()` (still **987**, #987's fast create had not yet incremented the chain counter). `predicted = alloc_predicted_job_id(987)` → **988** (local allocator). `create_job_fast` → `submit_fast` returns **Err** here (pool/priority), so the code enters its bounded **2 s confirm loop**, polls `get_next_job_id()`: it observes `after = 988` (only **#987's** increment) → `real = after-1 = 987` → `987 != predicted 988` → the anti-sibling-race guard `id == predicted` **wrongly rejects the confirmation** → `(false, predicted)` → AP returns `[gpn error] could not create job`. But **#988's own extrinsic landed just after** the loop (node block 81907 @09:02:58 `extrinsics_count: 5`), so the chain got job #988 — which AP now believes failed, so AP **never wrote the assignment** → provider never claims → **orphan Pending**. i.e. a **false-negative create confirmation under back-to-back fast submits**: the confirm compares against a counter delta (`after-1`) instead of looking up the job AP actually created (`predicted`).
**WHY:** user-facing OpenAI-compatible calls can return `could not create job` for a job that in fact landed, and each occurrence leaks a Pending job toward the global `MaxActiveJobs` cap (same failure class as documented open items (a)/(c) — now reproduced with exact evidence: `chatcmpl-gpn-988`).
**FIX: none applied this run** (per ROOT-CAUSE-FIRST + REVIEW-PROTOCOL: a code change requires MAIN APPROVE; brute-forcing is disallowed). **PROPOSAL (small, reversible, needs review+build):**
- **WHAT:** in `gpn_scheduler.rs` fast-AP confirm loop, confirm by **directly looking up the predicted id on-chain** (`client.get_job_state(predicted)` exists → `(true, predicted)`) instead of requiring `after-1 == predicted`; keep the 2 s bound.
- **WHY:** `predicted` is uniquely allocated to this request by the process-local allocator (`alloc_predicted_job_id`, never behind the chain counter), so a direct existence check is correct and avoids the off-by-one caused by a sibling's increment being observed in the counter delta.
- **RISK:** low — AP-only, read-only confirmation; no chain/genesis/wasm change; worst case falls back to today's behaviour.
- **ROLLBACK:** `git revert <commit>` (env flags untouched), rebuild `gpn_scheduler`.
**Open (unchanged, + this now evidence-backed):** (a) fast-AP create-confirm / `submit_fast` retry-once+nonce-refresh (THIS run's #988, plus #709/#719/#720); (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8) + #711 race; (c) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed; (d) explorer subxt metadata-refresh; (d2) GET /jobs O(n) sequential RPC (~15s); (e) item 6 Windows; (f) mainnet parity. **Root-cause-first (evidence):** At **12:03:18** the node binary `gpn-node/target/release/gpn-node` was **rebuilt** and the whole fleet restarted; on-chain runtime `state_getRuntimeVersion` is now **specVersion 8** (was 7 at 11:41 — `expire_job`/item 8 landed). **Fix (ONE, targeted):** restarted **only `gpn-accesspoint`** (not the provider/node) so it re-fetched spec-8 metadata from the now-stable node. Justified by root cause (stale startup metadata), not a reflex.
**Result / verify:** `/jobs` restored → **total 656 {Completed 427, Cancelled 222, Failed 1, Pending 6}**; `/job/655` now returns the real job (state Pending, requestor=hot wallet). Fresh probe via AP :8092 /v1/chat/completions (llama3.2:1b) → HTTP 200 coherent answer, **WALL 1.29s warm** (<5s MET); provider `claiming LLM job **#657**` == AP id (desync gone), 3 claims in the window (no storm). **Remaining:** Pending = pre-existing {305,306,345,568} + NEW {655,656} from the 2 broken-window probes; the AP's in-memory janitor can't reclaim them (set cleared at restart, and they were tracked under predicted ids 0/1) — spec-8 `expire_job` (`eeb3cdd`, now live) is the designed permissionless remedy; **escalating to Jeremy**. ## 2026-10-06 06:30 PT — monitor run: ROOT-CAUSED id-desync in fast-AP path (new orphan #568). No code deploy this run (needs MAIN review + build).

**Symptom observed:** this run's probe (POST /v1/chat/completions) returned HTTP 200 but body `[gpn error] could not create job` (2.07s). An immediate single retry succeeded (real 425-char answer). `/jobs` then showed a NEW on-chain Pending orphan **#568** (requestor 0xa8d7… = AP hot wallet, provider null).

**Evidence (root cause, not a guess):**
- The AP's fast path predicts the new job id from a **process-local** counter `NEXT_JOB_ID` (`alloc_predicted_job_id`, gpn_scheduler.rs:106). That counter advances on **every** request *before* the create is attempted — even when `create_job_fast` returns `Err` and the confirm window (`get_next_job_id` for ≤2s) never observes the id land.
- Measured offset: after the 06:21 probe's create was dropped, the AP's predicted ids are permanently **+1 vs the chain**. Proof from this run: AP predicted ids **570/571** for two concurrent probes while the chain assigned **569/570** (`/jobs` max 570 ⇒ chain `NextJobId`=571). Earlier: AP predicted 569 → chain created 568 (AP 569 = chain 568). Before 06:21 the two matched (566/567 = 566/567).
- Consequence: AP-keyed `llm_store.json` / `assignments.json` ids no longer match on-chain ids, so the on-chain job under the lower id is never matched by the provider/reconcile → stays **Pending** (orphan), and the caller intermittently gets `could not create job`.
- Not the cause: `MaxActiveJobs`=100 (only 4 Pending); tx pool empty; node/fleet mining normally; AP balance 1833.69 GPN (nonce 1204), provider capacity 4/4 free; no journal errors/panics; no hang.

**WHY no code change applied this run (WHAT/WHY/RISK/ROLLBACK — for MAIN review):**
- WHAT: on the fast-AP *unconfirmed* branch, re-seed the local allocator to the chain (`*NEXT_JOB_ID = get_next_job_id()`) so the next prediction realigns, and call `record_created(predicted,512)` so the janitor can reap a late-landing job.
- WHY: fixes the root cause (permanent +N desync after any dropped create) instead of the symptom.
- RISK: touches the **APPROVED/ON** fast path (changing logic, not defaults); under genuine concurrency a backward re-seed could momentarily collide with an in-flight prediction.
- ROLLBACK: revert the small diff; fast path otherwise unchanged.
- Deploy needs a clean `cargo build --release` of `gpn-agent` (no `target/` here ⇒ full build) + `gpn-accesspoint` restart — heavier/riskier than a monitor should do unreviewed.
- **Explicitly NOT done:** no service restart (no hang/deadlock; a restart would only be a workaround AND would drop the janitor's in-memory tracked-Pending set → more orphans).

**Result this run:** services active (AP PID 1110762, provider PID 1098961 ~16.5h); node mining (fleet head ~62989); tx pool drained; ~1 claim/request (5 claims/15min, no storm); e2e (retry) 6.08s — **over the <5s target**; residual Pending = {305,306,345 (pre-existing) + **568 (new, from this desync)**}. Escalating to Jeremy/MAIN for the reviewed fix; the desync will keep leaking ~1 on-chain Pending per probe until the allocator is realigned.

## 2026-10-06 13:54 PT — monitor run: FIXED (AP/provider chain clients stranded by the upgrade restart)
**Trigger (root-cause-first):** on-chain runtime was upgraded **spec 8 → 9** out-of-band ~13:45 (coinbase/block-producer reward; genesis `0xb8e4636a…` + canonical WASM `d9d2b536…` **unchanged** ⇒ forkless, no chain reset). During the upgrade the node self-shut down **twice** ("Essential task `txpool-background` failed", 13:45:03 + 13:46:05) → systemd `Restart=always` restarted **all** gpn units at the **same instant**.
**Cause (proved):** `gpn-accesspoint`+`gpn-provider` came up at 13:46:05, but the node's JSON-RPC server only bound at 13:46:06 → their subxt `OnlineClient` initialized before RPC was ready and **never recovered** (no retry). Symptom: AP `/jobs` → `{"jobs":[]}`, every `/job/{id}` → `not found` (incl. on-chain-existing #305), and `get_next_job_id`→**0** (real on-chain `NextJobId`=685) — i.e. the AP could neither READ nor CREATE on-chain jobs; e2e was served purely off-chain (probe ids `chatcmpl-gpn-0/1/2`, chain `NextJobId` did **not** advance).
**Fix (one, targeted, reversible):** ordered restart of `gpn-accesspoint`+`gpn-provider` at 13:52:16 **after** the node was up + stable (node not touched).
**Verified:** AP reads chain again — `/job/305` = `Pending` (requestor `0xa8d7…`, 512 GPN escrow); `/jobs` total **685** {Completed 434, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, Pending 9}; id prediction correct (**686**); new jobs land on-chain; e2e probe #686 HTTP200 coherent **WALL 1.33s (<5s MET)**; node head advancing 68330→68338, `NRestarts=0`, 0 new `txpool-background` failures. Pending = baseline {305,306,345,568,655,656} + transient probes {683,684,685}. Fast flags intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1`).
**OBSERVATION / flag:** repo `runtime/src/lib.rs` declares `spec_version: 6` while the live chain is **9**, and the built WASM (13:43) is spec-6 ⇒ the spec-9 runtime was built/deployed from **outside** this tree. Repo↔chain version **drift** — a future rebuild+set_code from HEAD would be a **downgrade**. Needs reconciliation + MAIN/Jeremy ack. STATUS GREEN (recovered) / queue item (8) still open.

## 2026-10-06 13:58 PT — monitor run: FIXED (full-stack simultaneous restart re-stranded AP/provider chain clients)
**Trigger (root-cause-first):** at **13:55:15 ALL gpn user units were stopped+started simultaneously** (journal: `Stopping gpn-accesspoint/diffusion/explorer/faucet/provider/fleet` then starts; node JSON-RPC only bound at **13:55:22**) — ~3 min after the 13:52:16 ordered fix. Actor not identifiable from available logs (no OpenClaw session other than this cron; NOT `topup-ap.sh` and NOT `run-fleet.sh` — neither restarts units). Looks like an external/manual `systemctl --user restart gpn.target`.
**Cause (proved):** AP/provider `subxt OnlineClient` initialised at 13:55:15, i.e. **before** node RPC bound at 13:55:22 → stranded, no retry (same failure mode as the 13:45 upgrade). Symptom: AP `/jobs` → `{"jobs":[]}` and `/job/305` → `not found` while on-chain state has 687 jobs and #305 = `Pending` (requestor `0xa8d7…`, 512 GPN escrow); AP create failed (`[gpn error] could not create job`); e2e survived only via GPN_FAST_AP off-chain path.
**Fix (one, targeted, reversible):** ordered `systemctl --user restart gpn-accesspoint gpn-provider` at **13:56:45**, after the node was up + stable (node untouched).
**Verified:** AP reads chain again — `/job/305`=`Pending`, `/jobs` total **687** {Completed 436, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, Pending 9}; e2e probe HTTP200 coherent **WALL 1.28s (<5s MET)** landed on-chain (id advanced → 688); exactly 1 claim/request, txpool `[]`, node head 0x10b0d→0x10b14 advancing, `NRestarts=0`. Pending = baseline {305,306,345,568,656} + restart-era transients {683,684,685,687} ⇒ **no new persistent orphan**. Fast flags intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` / `GPN_FAST_AP=1`).
**FLAG/RISK (needs Jeremy):** both the 13:45 upgrade and this 13:55:15 event restarted the **whole** gpn stack at once ⇒ AP/provider chain clients strand whenever they start before the node RPC is ready. Recommend a readiness gate (`ExecStartPre` wait-for-RPC, or `After=` the node unit) so a stack-wide restart cannot re-strand them — and identify what issued the 13:55:15 simultaneous restart. STATUS GREEN (recovered).

## 2026-10-06 14:01 PT / 21:01 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — VERIFY + ROOT-CAUSE; GREEN; e2e MET; no change applied (review channel down)
**Services (all healthy ⇒ no restart):** 9 gpn units loaded/active + `gpn-ap-topup.timer` waiting. `gpn-provider` PID 1321305 + `gpn-accesspoint` PID 1321304 active since 13:56:45, `NRestarts=0`. `journalctl --user -u gpn-provider`/`-u gpn-accesspoint` 20m = **0 errors/panics/fatal/deadlock/timeout**; jobs #688/#689 clean claim→done. `GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` (~/.gpn/provider.env) + `GPN_FAST_AP=1` (~/.gpn/accesspoint.env) intact. Node head `0x10b26` (~68390) advancing; txpool 5 pending (expected heartbeat/topup extrinsics). On-chain **specVersion=10** (`impl=gpn`). Canonical runtime WASM content md5 `d9d2b5369dcb11b42da466687c0f4077` **UNCHANGED** — but now **UNTRACKED in git** (see obs (i)).
**Probe:** POST /v1/chat/completions AP :8092 (llama3.2:1b, ~250-char forkless-upgrade/salted-commitment prompt) → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-689`, coherent 661-char answer, **WALL 1.87s (<5s MET)**. Exactly **1 claim/request** (#689 @13:59:25→done :26) = no storm, no id desync.
**Jobs:** AP GET /jobs total 688 {Completed 437, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, **Pending 9**} = baseline {305,306,345,568,656} + restart-era probes {683,684,685,687} ⇒ **no new persistent orphan**. `/job/305`=`Pending` (requestor 0xa8d7…, 512 GPN escrow) ⇒ AP chain-read OK.
**ROOT CAUSE (proven — recurring full-stack restart ⇒ AP/provider chain-client stranding):** (1) every app unit (`gpn-accesspoint/provider/diffusion/explorer/faucet`) declares **`Requires=gpn-fleet.service`**; systemd propagates stop to `Requires=` dependents, so a single fleet bounce stops+starts the whole stack in the same second (journal 13:45:03 / 13:46:05 / 13:55:15 = "Stopping gpn-fleet" + all apps). (2) At 13:55:15 fleet started but the **node JSON-RPC only bound at 13:55:22** (7 s later); AP/provider build their `subxt OnlineClient` at 13:55:15 with **no readiness retry** ⇒ stranded (AP `/jobs`=[], create fails, `get_next_job_id`=0) — identical to the 13:45 upgrade strand; e2e survived only via the off-chain `GPN_FAST_AP` path. (3) The "Essential task `txpool-background` failed" line is a **symptom** of the stop (always adjacent to systemd "Stopping gpn-fleet"), not the cause. The identity of the restart issuer remains unknown (no systemd audit logging) — needs `LogLevel=debug`/audit to pin, but the strand mechanism is independent of who issues the restart.
**PROPOSED FIX (one, targeted, reversible — NOT applied; needs MAIN APPROVE):** WHAT: add an RPC-readiness gate for the RPC-dependent units — `ExecStartPre=/home/jeremyg/.gpn/wait-for-rpc.sh` (poll `system_health` on :9944 until up, ≤30 s) in `gpn-accesspoint.service` + `gpn-provider.service`; optionally relax `Requires=`→`Wants=` for the apps so a node restart no longer bounces them. WHY: removes the init-before-RPC race = root cause above. RISK: low — unit-file only, no runtime/genesis. ROLLBACK: `git checkout` the unit files (or drop ExecStartPre) + `systemctl --user daemon-reload`. BLOCKED: MAIN reviewer UNREACHABLE (`sessions_list` = ONLY this cron, kind=other) ⇒ cannot self-approve per REVIEW-PROTOCOL; flagged to Jeremy.
**OBSERVATIONS (not acted on):** (i) commit `c1e199e` (mislabeled "docs(change-log)") bundled live source edits (`node/src/service.rs`, `pallets/gpu-pow/src/lib.rs`, `runtime/src/lib.rs` `spec_version 6→10`) **and deleted the canonical tracked runtime WASM**. Disk file md5 still equals the pre-deletion tracked blob (d9d2b536…) ⇒ **no corruption**, but the overwrite safeguard (tracked artifact) is gone. Recommend re-tracking `gpn-node/runtime/gpn_runtime.compact.compressed.wasm` (needs review). (ii) **spec drift persists:** on-chain specVersion **10**; HEAD `runtime/src/lib.rs` = **6** (9cbb89e reverted 10→6 as a side effect). A rebuild+set_code from HEAD would be a **downgrade** ⇒ reconcile before any deploy. (iii) Queue: (6) Windows rejoin NEEDS JEREMY; (8) `expire_job` runtime upgrade needs MAIN+Root; (9) mainnet future. Queue **blocked, not empty** ⇒ cron 95b27deb stays ENABLED.

**Trigger/root-cause (proven):** A **forkless on-chain runtime upgrade fired at 15:28:36** — node (`gpn-fleet`, NRestarts=0, PID 1320729 up since 13:55:15) logged `ComputeMarketplace declares internal migrations` + `gpn-marketplace: capacity migration pruned terminal jobs` **without restarting**; on-chain `state_getRuntimeVersion` went **spec 10 → 11** (commit `be390db` "feat(pow)…" bumped `runtime/src/lib.rs spec_version: 11`). The long-lived AP+provider `subxt OnlineClient`s were connected with **cached spec-10 metadata**, so their **first submit after the upgrade was rejected** by the tx-pool with `InvalidTransaction 1010 "Transaction has a bad signature"` (signature payload carries the stale spec_version). **Fix (targeted, NOT applied — needs rebuild+restart ⇒ MAIN APPROVE):** in `chain/signer.rs::submit_fast`, on Err do **one** immediate reconnect + retry of the same call (the stream drop already refreshes spec metadata) so the first post-upgrade request succeeds instead of surfacing an error/orphan; optionally raise the AP create-confirm window above one PoW block. ## 2026-10-06 15:50 PT / 22:50 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — FAULT FOUND: client-side janitor cancelled a job the provider had already served (#711) → work done but job Cancelled (provider unpaid, escrow refunded); cause = known job-lifecycle gap (queue item 8); NO restart, NO change applied (fix = runtime upgrade ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE)
**Root-cause-first (evidence):** on-chain `GET /job/711` now reads **state=Cancelled**, yet `journalctl -u gpn-provider` shows the provider legitimately processed it: `claiming LLM job #711` 15:41:04 → `[job 711] done — 546 chars` 15:41:05. The AP janitor then claimed it: `[janitor] cancel #711 failed: ServerError(1014) "Priority is too low: (2424 vs 2362)"` 15:43:07, then `[janitor] cancelled stale Pending #711 (max_tokens 256, waited >120s)` 15:44:07. So the job sat **Pending** on-chain >120 s after the provider served it (its fast-path `accept_job` tx never landed = the documented "fast-path leak" that `process_once` heals via `Pending→Accepted→…→Completed`), the token-scaled janitor timeout (`stale_secs(256)=60+(256/20)*2=84→clamp 120s`) fired first, and the **cancel won the race against the provider's reconcile/heal** (#710/#712/#713 all healed cleanly; #711 did not). NET: real economic-correctness bug — provider did the inference, job Cancelled ⇒ provider unpaid, requester escrow refunded. This is exactly the client-side-janitor-vs-on-chain-expiry gap (changes-lost race) described in `shared_workspace/artifacts/project7/job-lifecycle-gap-analysis-2026-10-05.md`.
**Secondary (transient, self-healed):** the 15:43:07 janitor tx error (1014 priority-too-low) = one-shot tx-pool priority collision from the shared AP signer (`cancel_job_fast` same-nonce vs an in-pool tx); it retried next tick and succeeded — no orphan, not recurring.
**Fix (targeted, NOT applied — NOT a restart):** retire the client-side janitor in favour of a **pallet permissionless expire/timeout** (queue item 8; source/tests exist per 2026-10-05 note) so expiry is ordered on-chain and cannot race the provider's completion/reconcile; secondarily make the provider heal Pending-leaks more eagerly than the janitor's stale window. Rejected blind alternatives: service restart (would drop the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans; services alive, `NRestarts=0`) and any fast-path flag change (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1` APPROVED-ON, not reverted). Runtime change ⇒ **cannot apply without MAIN APPROVE; MAIN reviewer UNREACHABLE** (`sessions_list` = ONLY this cron).

## 2026-10-07 10:53 PT / 17:53 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — ANOMALY (10th): phantom #1025 + PROVEN fast-AP id-desync (AP predicted 1026, chain job = 1025); root cause = pool-ack treated as landed create; no fix (needs MAIN APPROVE), no restart
**Root-cause-first (evidence gathered BEFORE any action):** unit state + provider journal + AP /jobs are healthy; the fault is in the fast-AP create path. No hang/deadlock in any log ⇒ **no restart** (restart drops the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans). Fast-path flags APPROVED-ON and intact/live (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` provider.env + PID 1321305 environ; `GPN_FAST_AP=1` accesspoint.env + PID 1380823 environ); not reverted.
**Probe #1 (text):** POST /v1/chat/completions :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-1025`, coherent 297-char answer, **WALL 4.88s (<5s MET)**; provider claimed #1025 10:48:45 → done :48 (inference ran), 1 claim/request. BUT on-chain: `NextJobId=1025` (StorageValue 0x0401) stuck for **>4 min**, `Jobs[1025]` **ABSENT**, `author_pendingExtrinsics` **EMPTY**, blocks 83191-83220 all `extrinsics_count:1 / NoMoreTransactions`. ⇒ **phantom**: AP returned an id + ran inference, but NO on-chain create (same class as #1003-#1016/#1023/#1024). Not a cap/balance issue: `ActiveJobCount=11` (max 100), AP hot wallet `5Ft5q2AViqtBrnaZmUp86KeQDuwczo2KHze5MR99892hVd9B` free 1601.4 GPN (escrow = 0.512 GPN/job).
**DECISIVE drift proof — Probe #2 (immediately after):** POST :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-**1026**` "pong"; live pool transiently 3-5 txs, `NextJobId 1025→1026` at t≈12s. Now `Jobs[1025]` **FOUND**, `Jobs[1026]` **ABSENT** ⇒ the on-chain job created for probe #2 is id **1025**, while the AP labelled it **1026** and wrote assignments.json `1026`. **⇒ AP predicted id is off-by-one from the real on-chain id.**
**Actual root cause (code):** `gpn_scheduler.rs` fast path (`GPN_FAST_AP`) predicts the id via the process-local `alloc_predicted_job_id(next_before)=max(local,chain_next)` and submits via `create_job_fast`→`submit_fast`→`signer::NonceStream`→`submit_signed_no_wait`, which returns `Ok` on a **tx-pool ACK** and **deliberately ignores the dispatch outcome**. So when a `create_job` tx is dropped or fails at dispatch, the AP still sets `created=true` with the predicted id (phantom), and the local counter advances ⇒ allocator drifts +1 ahead of chain `NextJobId` ⇒ every later request's predicted id ≠ its real on-chain id. (The reconcile/self-heal covers accept/start/result/complete lanes, but there is NO reconcile for the create.)
**Services:** gpn-accesspoint PID 1380823 active (up 15h, NRestarts=0), gpn-provider PID 1321305 active (up 20h, NRestarts=0), gpn-fleet node best #83220 advancing, AP :8092 `/health` {providers:1, ok}, provider :8093 ok. journalctl provider fault-grep (panic/fatal/deadlock/error/1010/1014) last 25m = **0 hits**.
**Blocked/open (unchanged, need MAIN APPROVE/JEREMY; MAIN session not visible to this cron ⇒ unreachable):** (a) `submit_fast` retry-once/nonce-refresh + **AP create-confirm window** (verify `NextJobId` advanced before declaring `created`; else re-seed allocator from chain + retry once) — THIS is the fix for the phantom; (d) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed (make predicted id self-correct when it drifts). Touches the APPROVED fast path ⇒ cannot apply without approval. (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8); (c) RPC-readiness gate; (e) diffusion silent-wedge instrumentation; (f) item 6 Windows; (g) mainnet parity. STATUS: CORE GREEN, ANOMALY PRESENT / NOT DONE.
